-You can use the Language Model API in different types of extensions. For example, the Rust extension might use the Language Model to offer default names to improve its rename experience.
+Use the Language Model API to add model-powered behavior to a [language](/api/language-extensions/overview) or [debugger](/api/extension-guides/debugger-extension) extension, or to an extension [command](/api/extension-guides/command) or [task](/api/extension-guides/task-provider). To extend Copilot chat workflows, start with [agent plugins or tools](/api/extension-guides/ai/ai-extensibility-overview.md#extend-the-chat-experience) instead of implementing a chat participant.

```diff
--- microsoft_vscode-docs::api/extension-guides/ai/chat.md (prev: 7b986c0b)
+++ microsoft_vscode-docs::api/extension-guides/ai/chat.md (new: a5fe3119)
@@ -1,586 +1,25 @@
-**MetaDescription**: A guide to creating an AI extension in Visual Studio Code
+**MetaDescription**: Replace deprecated chat participants with agent plugins, MCP tools and Apps, or editor-specific extension tools.
 
-# Chat Participant API
+# Chat participants (deprecated)
 
-Chat participants are specialized assistants that enable users to extend [chat in VS Code](/docs/chat/chat-overview) with domain-specific experts. Users invoke a chat participant by @-mentioning it, and the participant is then responsible for handling the user's natural language prompt.
+Chat participants are deprecated and are not supported in the Copilot harness. The Chat Participant API remains available for Local harness compatibility. Do not use it for new Copilot integrations.
 
-In this extension guide, you learn how to create a chat participant by using the Chat Participant API.
+For an existing participant, follow [Replace chat participants](/docs/agent-customization/migrate-customizations.md#replace-chat-participants). The guide covers migration for both people who use a participant and extension authors who maintain one.
 
-VS Code has several built-in chat participants like `@vscode`, `@terminal`, or `@workspace`. They are optimized to answer questions about their respective domains.
+## Choose a replacement
 
-Chat participants are different from [language model tools](/api/extension-guides/ai/tools) that are invoked as part of the LLM orchestrating the steps needed to resolve the user's chat prompt. Chat participants receive the user's prompt and orchestrate the tasks that are needed themselves.
+Start with an [agent plugin](/docs/agent-customization/agent-plugins.md) that packages skills and an MCP server:
 
-## Why implement a chat participant in your extension?
+* **Skills** provide domain knowledge and reusable workflows that users can invoke as slash commands.
+* **MCP tools** retrieve data and perform actions.
+* **MCP Apps** provide interactive controls, including buttons, forms, and multi-step workflows. See the [MCP development guide](/api/extension-guides/ai/mcp.md#mcp-apps).
+* **Custom agents** optionally provide an ongoing specialist role.
 
-Implementing a chat participant in your extension has several benefits:
+Use [extension tools](/api/extension-guides/ai/tools.md) when the integration needs live editor context or access to VS Code APIs. For information the user can supply directly, use [chat context attachments](/docs/chat/copilot-chat-context.md).
 
-- **Extend chat** with specialized, domain-specific knowledge and expertise. For example, the built-in `@vscode` participant has knowledge about VS Code and its extension APIs.
-- **Own conversation** by managing the end-to-end user chat prompt and response.
-- **Deeply integrate with VS Code** by using the broad set of extension APIs. For example, use the [debug APIs](/api/extension-guides/debugger-extension) to get the current debugging context and use it as part of the tool's functionality.
-- **Distribute and deploy** chat participants via the Visual Studio Marketplace, providing a reliable and seamless experience for users. Users don't need a separate installation and update process.
+There is no automatic conversion of a participant's request handler, history, feedback events, or response UI. Rebuild interactive UI as an MCP App where appropriate; app-level confirmation does not replace host tool approvals. Keep bespoke model orchestration or editor UI in extension commands and views when it does not fit a tool-based workflow.
 
-You might consider implementing a [language model tool](/api/extension-guides/ai/tools) or [MCP server](/api/extension-guides/ai/mcp) if you want to provide domain-specific capabilities that can be invoked automatically as part of an autonomous, agentic coding session. See the [AI Extensibility Overview](/api/extension-guides/ai/ai-extensibility-overview) for details on the different options and how to decide which approach to use.
+## Related resources
 
-## Parts of the chat user experience
-
-The following screenshot shows the different chat concepts in the Visual Studio Code chat experience for the sample extension.
-
-![Chat concepts explanation](../images/ai/chat/chat.png)
-
-1. Use the `@` syntax to invoke the `@cat` chat participant
-1. Use the `/` syntax to call the `/teach` command
-1. User-provided query, also known as the user prompt
-1. Icon and participant `fullName` that indicate that Copilot is using the `@cat` chat participant
-1. Markdown response, provided by `@cat`
-1. Code fragment included in the markdown response
-1. Button included in the `@cat` response, the button invokes a VS Code command
-1. Suggested [follow-up questions](#4-register-follow-up-requests) provided by the chat participant
-1. Chat input field with the placeholder text provided by the chat participant's `description` property
-
-## Create a chat participant
-
-Implementing a chat participant consists of the following parts:
-
-1. Define the chat participant in the `package.json` file of your extension.
-1. Implement a request handler to process the user's chat prompt and return a response.
-1. (Optional) Implement chat slash commands to provide users with a shorthand notation for common tasks.
-1. (Optional) Define suggested follow-up questions.
-1. (Optional) Implement participant detection where VS Code automatically routes a chat request to the appropriate chat participant without explicit mention from the user.
-
-You can get started with a [basic example project](https://github.com/microsoft/vscode-extension-samples/tree/main/chat-sample).
-
-![Diagram showing how extension can contribute to chat](../images/ai/chat/diagram.png)
-
-### 1. Register the chat participant
-
-The first step to create a chat extension is to register it in your `package.json`, with the following properties:
-
-- `id`: A unique identifier for the chat participant, as defined in the `package.json` file.
-- `name`: A short name for the chat participant, used for @-mentions in the chat.
-- `fullName`: The full name of the chat participant, displayed in the title area of the response.
-- `description`: A brief description of the chat participant's purpose, used as placeholder text in the chat input field.
-- `isSticky`: A boolean value indicating whether the chat participant is persistent in the chat input field after responding.
-
-```json
-"contributes": {
-        "chatParticipants": [
-            {
-                "id": "chat-sample.my-participant",
-                "name": "my-participant",
-                "fullName": "My Participant",
-                "description": "What can I teach you?",
-                "isSticky": true
-            }
-        ]
-}
-```
-
-We suggest using a lowercase `name` and using title case for the `fullName` to align with existing chat participants. Get more info about the [naming conventions for chat participants](#chat-participant-naming-conventions).
-
-> [!NOTE]
-> Some participant names are reserved. If you use such a reserved name, VS Code displays the fully qualified name of your chat participant (including the extension ID).
-
-### 2. Implement a request handler
-
-Implement the chat participant by using the [Chat Participant API](/api/references/vscode-api#chat). This consists of the following steps:
-
-1. On activation of the extension, create the participant with `vscode.chat.createChatParticipant`.
-
-    Provide the ID, which you defined in `package.json`, and a reference to the request handler that you implement in the next step.
-
-    ```typescript
-    export function activate(context: vscode.ExtensionContext) {
-
-        // Register the chat participant and its request handler
-        const cat = vscode.chat.createChatParticipant('chat-sample.my-participant', handler);
-
-        // Optionally, set some properties for @cat
-        cat.iconPath = vscode.Uri.joinPath(context.extensionUri, 'cat.jpeg');
-
-        // Add the chat request handler here
-    }
-    ```
-
-1. In the `activate` function, define the `vscode.ChatRequestHandler` request handler.
```

### [MODIFICADO] `microsoft_vscode-docs::api/extension-guides/ai/chat-tutorial.md`

- **Hash Nuevo**: `00ed6f4b4ac28ca7f9147ba12226dd73b503884a9c5006ba4ba9665139ac6113`

```diff
--- microsoft_vscode-docs::api/extension-guides/ai/chat-tutorial.md (prev: 5cc0c151)
+++ microsoft_vscode-docs::api/extension-guides/ai/chat-tutorial.md (new: 00ed6f4b)
@@ -1,346 +1,15 @@
-**MetaDescription**: Tutorial that walks you through creating a GitHub Copilot chat participant in VS Code by using the Chat API.
+**MetaDescription**: Migrate a code-tutor chat participant to an agent plugin with skills, MCP tools, and interactive exercises.
 
-# Tutorial: Build a code tutorial chat participant with the Chat API
+# Code-tutor chat participant tutorial (deprecated)
 
-In this tutorial, you'll learn how to create a Visual Studio Code extension that integrates with the GitHub Copilot Chat experience. You'll use the Chat extension API to contribute a chat participant. Your participant will be a code tutor that can provide explanations and sample exercises for programming concepts.
+Chat participants are deprecated and are not supported in the Copilot harness. This tutorial has been retired. The Chat Participant API remains available for Local harness compatibility.
 
-## Prerequisites
+For the supported approach, follow [Migrate a code tutor to a plugin](/docs/agent-customization/migrate-customizations.md#example-migrate-a-code-tutor-to-a-plugin). Package teaching workflows as skills and exercise operations as MCP tools. Use MCP Apps for interactive exercises, and extension tools when you need live editor context.
 
-You'll need the following tools and accounts to complete this tutorial:
+The migration guide explains which participant responsibilities have replacements and which require redesign. Renaming a participant or copying its request handler into a custom-agent file does not migrate the integration.
 
-- [Visual Studio Code](https://code.visualstudio.com/download)
-- [GitHub Copilot](https://marketplace.visualstudio.com/items?itemName=GitHub.copilot-chat)
-- [Node.js](https://nodejs.org/en/download/)
+## Related resources
 
-## Step 1: Set up your project
-
-First, generate the extension project by using Yeoman and the VS Code Extension Generator.
-
-```bash
-npx --package yo --package generator-code -- yo code
-```
-
-Select the following options to complete the setup:
-
-```bash
-# ? What type of extension do you want to create? New Extension (TypeScript)
-# ? What's the name of your extension? Code Tutor
-
-### Press <Enter> to choose default for all options below ###
-
-# ? What's the identifier of your extension? code-tutor
-# ? What's the description of your extension? LEAVE BLANK
-# ? Initialize a git repository? Yes
-# ? Bundle the source code with webpack? No
-# ? Which package manager to use? npm
-
-# ? Do you want to open the new folder with Visual Studio Code? Open with `code`
-```
-
-Once your extension project is generated, there are two files you will be working in: `extension.ts` and `package.json`, which you can learn more about in the [Extension Anatomy docs](/api/get-started/extension-anatomy#extension-file-structure). As a quick overview:
-
-- `extension.ts` is the main entry point for your extension and contains the logic for your chat participant.
-- `package.json` contains the metadata for your extension, such as the name and description of your participant.
-
-Delete the auto-generated code in the `extension.ts` `activate()` method. This is where you will place our logic for our chat participant.
-
-## Step 2: Register a Chat participant
-
-In the `package.json` file, replace the auto-generated `contributes` section with the following:
-
-```json
-"contributes":{
-    "chatParticipants": [
-    {
-        "id": "chat-tutorial.code-tutor",
-        "fullName": "Code Tutor",
-        "name": "tutor",
-        "description": "What can I teach you?",
-        "isSticky": true
-    }
-    ]
-}
-```
-
-This code registers a chat participant with the following attributes:
-
-- Unique ID `chat-tutorial.code-tutor`, which will be referenced in the code
-- Full name `Code Tutor`, which will be shown in the title area of a response from your participant
-- Name `tutor`, which will be used to reference the chat participant as `@tutor` in the Chat view
-- Description "What can I teach you?", which will be shown in the chat input field as a placeholder text
-
-Finally, setting `isSticky: true` will automatically prepend the participant name in the chat input field after the user has started interacting with the participant.
-
-## Step 3: Craft the prompt
-
-Now that the participant is registered, you can start implementing the logic for the code tutor. In the `extension.ts` file, you will define a prompt for the requests.
-
-Crafting a good prompt is the key to getting the best response from your participant. Check out [this article](https://platform.openai.com/docs/guides/prompt-engineering) for tips on prompt engineering.
-
-Your code tutor should emulate a real-world tutor by guiding the student to understand the concept instead of providing direct answers. Additionally, the tutor should remain focused on the topic and refrain from answering non-programming questions.
-
-Consider the following two prompts. Which is more likely to give the specified behavior?
-
-1. > You are a helpful code tutor. Your job is to teach the user with simple descriptions and sample code of the concept.
-2. > You are a helpful code tutor. Your job is to teach the user with simple descriptions and sample code of the concept. Respond with a guided overview of the concept in a series of messages. Do not give the user the answer directly, but guide them to find the answer themselves. If the user asks a non-programming question, politely decline to respond.
-
-The second prompt is more specific and gives the participant a clear direction on how to respond. Add this prompt in the `extension.ts` file.
-
-```ts
-const BASE_PROMPT = 'You are a helpful code tutor. Your job is to teach the user with simple descriptions and sample code of the concept. Respond with a guided overview of the concept in a series of messages. Do not give the user the answer directly, but guide them to find the answer themselves. If the user asks a non-programming question, politely decline to respond.';
-```
-
-## Step 4: Implement the request handler
-
-Now that the prompt is selected, you need to implement the request handler. This is what will process the user's chat request. You will define the request handler, perform logic for processing the request, and return a response to the user.
-
-First, define the handler:
-
-```ts
-// define a chat handler
-const handler: vscode.ChatRequestHandler = async (request: vscode.ChatRequest, context: vscode.ChatContext, stream: vscode.ChatResponseStream, token: vscode.CancellationToken) => {
-
-    return;
-}
-```
-
-Within the body of this handler, initialize the prompt and a `messages` array with the prompt. Then, send in what the user typed in the chat box. You can access this through `request.prompt`.
-
-Send the request using `request.model.sendRequest`, which will send the request using the currently selected model. Finally, stream the response to the user.
-
-```ts
```

+For Copilot sessions, instructions are discovered at the repository root, the session's working directory, and intermediate directories between them. Copilot combines applicable instructions from `AGENTS.md` and other supported instruction files. Avoid conflicting guidance because there is no general precedence order between these files. For more information, see [Copilot instruction discovery](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/how-tos/copilot-cli/customize-copilot/add-custom-instructions).
+
+> [!NOTE]
+> **For Local harness sessions:** The ``chat.useAgentsMdFile`` setting controls whether instructions from workspace-root `AGENTS.md` files are automatically attached to chat requests and is enabled by default. This setting does not control instruction discovery in other harnesses.
 
 ### Use multiple `AGENTS.md` files
 
-`feature(nested-agents-md-files)`
-
-Use nested `AGENTS.md` files when different folders need different guidance. Follow the selected harness's working-folder and nested-instruction rules for discovery and activation.
-
-> [!NOTE]
-> **For Local harness sessions:** The ``chat.useNestedAgentsMdFiles`` setting controls support for nested `AGENTS.md` files and is disabled by default. When enabled, VS Code lists these files with their folder locations so the agent can load relevant instructions for the task.
+Use nested `AGENTS.md` files when different folders need different guidance. As Copilot works with files in subfolders, it discovers `AGENTS.md` files along their directory paths. Other harnesses use their own discovery and activation rules.
+
+> [!NOTE]
+> **For Local harness sessions:** `feature(nested-agents-md-files)` Set both ``chat.useAgentsMdFile`` and ``chat.useNestedAgentsMdFiles`` to `true` to discover nested `AGENTS.md` files. The nested-file setting is disabled by default. When both settings are enabled, VS Code lists these files with their folder locations so the agent can load relevant instructions for the task. Neither setting controls Copilot instruction discovery.
 
 > [!TIP]
 > For folder-specific instructions, you can also use multiple [`.instructions.md`](#use-instructionsmd-files) files with different `applyTo` patterns that match the folder structure.

- **URL Canónica**: https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/agent-customization/hooks
- **Hash Anterior**: `cf0b8eb526f3d21cbf3420ee8d412d6228c34eced8fff86b0ff6e209d5f35b5d`
- **Hash Nuevo**: `3e1b6ea3c537de279c642db3f7dfeba18817f97f7d51420fd9af10c74fd80c7f`

```diff
--- microsoft_vscode-docs::docs/agent-customization/hooks.md (prev: cf0b8eb5)
+++ microsoft_vscode-docs::docs/agent-customization/hooks.md (new: 3e1b6ea3)
@@ -1,89 +1,89 @@
-**MetaDescription**: Configure agent hooks in VS Code for the Copilot, Claude, Codex, and Local harnesses.
+**MetaDescription**: Configure Copilot hooks for personal and shared workflows in VS Code.
 
 # Configure agent hooks in Visual Studio Code (Preview)
 
-Hooks run custom actions at specific points in an agent's lifecycle. Use hooks to automate workflows, validate operations, create audit trails, or enforce policies independently of the language model.
-
-The selected [agent harness](/docs/agents/concepts/agent-harnesses.md) determines which hook implementation runs. Before you create or reuse a hook, identify the harness and use its configuration and event schema.
-
-This article helps you choose the correct hook implementation, manage hooks from VS Code, and configure hooks for the Local harness.
+Hooks run custom actions at specific points in an agent's lifecycle. Use them to add context, validate a tool call, run a formatter, or record an event without relying on the model to request the action.
+
+This article helps you configure hooks for the shared Copilot runtime, choose a personal or shared source, and verify the result. If you already have hooks, start with [Migrate hooks](/docs/agent-customization/migrate-customizations.md#migrate-hooks). Migration includes user folders, settings, custom agents, and plugins, not only repository files.
 
 > [!NOTE]
-> The VS Code hooks experience is in Preview. Individual provider implementations might have a different lifecycle status. For example, hooks in the [copilot.copilot_sdk_short] are generally available.
+> The VS Code hooks experience is in Preview. Individual provider implementations can have a different lifecycle status and release schedule.
 
 > [!IMPORTANT]
-> Your organization might restrict which hooks can run. Contact your administrator for more information. Administrators can learn how to [manage hooks in enterprise environments](/docs/enterprise/manage-ai-settings.md#enable-or-disable-hooks).
+> Your organization might restrict which hooks can run. Claude and Codex harnesses are disabled when [Copilot enterprise-managed settings apply](/docs/enterprise/manage-ai-settings.md#claude-and-codex-availability). Contact the IT team that deployed the settings to discuss access. Administrators can learn how to [manage hooks in enterprise environments](/docs/enterprise/manage-ai-settings.md#enable-or-disable-hooks).
 
 ## Choose the hook implementation for your session
 
-The **Session Target** UI control selects the agent harness. The harness owns the hook lifecycle and payloads.
-
-| **Session Target** choice | Hook implementation | Configuration and event reference |
-|----------------|---------------------|-----------------------------------|
-| **Copilot** | Shared [copilot.copilot_sdk_short] implementation | Use the [GitHub Copilot hooks reference](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/hooks-reference). |
-| **Claude** | Claude Agent SDK | Use the [Claude hooks reference](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks). |
-| **Codex** | Codex runtime | Use the [Codex hooks documentation](https://developers.openai.com/codex/hooks/). |
-| **Cloud** | Selected cloud agent | Use the provider documentation. For Copilot cloud agent, see the [GitHub Copilot hooks reference](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/hooks-reference). |
-
-> [!NOTE]
-> **For Local harness sessions:** Use the [Local configuration](#configure-hooks-for-the-local-harness) in this article and the [Local hooks reference](/docs/agents/reference/hooks-reference.md). These hooks have their own events, settings, and payloads.
-
-Some harnesses discover the same hook files, such as `.github/hooks/*.json` or `.claude/settings.json`. This file compatibility does not make their behavior identical. Supported events, event names, matchers, command properties, tool names, payloads, and output decisions can differ.
-
-Copilot harness sessions use the same SDK hook implementation as Copilot CLI. Use the Copilot CLI sections of the GitHub reference for runtime configuration and payloads, but verify that the event is available in the selected VS Code version.
+Select **Copilot** from the **Session Target** UI control to use the Copilot harness. VS Code, the GitHub Copilot app, and GitHub Copilot CLI share this harness and its hook format. You do not need to install or use the CLI to configure hooks in the editor.
+
+Use the [Copilot hooks reference](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/hooks-reference) for command configuration, event inputs, and outputs.
+
+Other session targets use their own hook implementations:
+
+| **Session Target** choice | Configuration and event reference |
+|--------------------------|-----------------------------------|
+| **Claude** | [Claude hooks reference](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks) |
+| **Codex** | [Codex hooks documentation](https://developers.openai.com/codex/hooks/) |
+| **Cloud** | The selected cloud provider's documentation. For Copilot cloud agent, use the cloud-agent sections of the GitHub reference for its execution environment and event coverage. |
+
+The older Local harness has a separate [Local hooks reference](/docs/agents/reference/hooks-reference.md). Use it to interpret existing hooks during migration, not as the schema for a new Copilot hook.
 
 ### Migrate hooks between harnesses
 
-Before you switch harnesses for an existing workflow:
-
-* Confirm that the destination harness discovers the hook file.
-* Compare the supported events and command properties.
-* Check tool names and input shapes before you reuse filters or validation logic.
-* Validate scripts that read chat transcripts. Transcript formats are not a stable cross-harness API.
-* Test output decisions, such as blocking a tool call or adding context.
-* Confirm where the hook command runs and which files and environment variables it can access.
-
-Do not validate a migration by changing only the language model. Select the destination harness from the **Session Target** UI control and start a test session.
+Sharing a hook file location does not establish runtime compatibility. Event naming can select a different payload, tool names and arguments can change, and output or error behavior can differ.
+
+Follow the [hook migration procedure and compatibility matrix](/docs/agent-customization/migrate-customizations.md#migrate-hooks) before reusing a hook. Select the destination harness and start a test session; changing only the language model does not test migration.
 
 ## Create and manage hooks
 
-Select the harness before you open the hooks interface. This ensures that VS Code shows the customizations for that harness.
-
-To view and manage hooks:
+Select the harness before opening the hooks interface so the editor shows the relevant customization sources.
 
 1. Open the Chat view or Agents window.
-
-1. Select the harness or Cloud target from the **Session Target** UI control.
-
+1. Select **Copilot** from the **Session Target** UI control.
 1. Open the Agent Customizations editor:
-
-    * In the Chat view, select **Open Customizations** in the title bar, and then select **Hooks**.
-    * In the Agents window, select **Hooks** in the **Customizations** panel.
-    * In the Chat view, with **Local** or **Copilot** selected, enter `/hooks` in the chat input.
-
-1. Review the hook sources for the selected harness or Cloud target. Select a hook to open its configuration.
-
-You can also run **Chat: Configure Hooks** from the Command Palette (``Shortcut: workbench.action.showCommands``) to manage Local hook files.
-
-To generate a hook with AI, describe the hook you want in chat. With **Local** selected, you can also enter `/create-hook <description>` in chat or run **Chat: Generate Hook** from the Command Palette. These shortcuts aren't available for other harnesses. The `/create-hook` command generates hooks in `.github/hooks/`. Review the result against the destination harness reference before you use it.
-
-For Claude and Codex, use the provider documentation for additional configuration and management options.
-
-## Configure hooks for the Local harness
-
-The following sections describe Local hook events, settings, payloads, and output decisions. These do not automatically apply to other harnesses.
-
-### Create your first Local hook
-
-The following hook records the name of every tool before the Local harness invokes it. Create `.github/hooks/audit.json` in your workspace:
+   * In the Chat view, select **Open Customizations** in the title bar, and then **Hooks**.
+   * In the Agents window, select **Hooks** in the **Customizations** panel.
+   * In the Chat view, enter `/hooks` in the chat input.
+1. Review the sources and select a hook to open its configuration.
+
+To generate a hook with AI, describe the hook you want in chat. The `/create-hook <description>` and **Chat: Generate Hook** shortcuts are available only for Local sessions. Review generated configuration and scripts against the destination reference before running them. Generation is not automatic migration or verification.
+
+## Choose a hook source
+
+Choose the scope that matches the workflow:
+
+| Need | Source |
+|------|--------|
+| Personal automation across repositories | User hook files in `~/.copilot/hooks/`, or `$COPILOT_HOME/hooks/` when `COPILOT_HOME` is set. |
+| Automation shared with a repository | `.github/hooks/*.json` at the repository root |
```

To reuse profile-based agents with another harness, [move them to that harness's user folder](/docs/agent-customization/migrate-customizations.md#move-user-agents-and-instructions).
 
 The deprecated ``chat.agentFilesLocations`` and ``chat.modeFilesLocations`` settings add other locations for the Local harness only. To reuse agents from these locations with the Copilot harness, [move them to supported locations](/docs/agent-customization/migrate-customizations.md#move-customizations-from-configured-locations).
+
+If an agent has `hooks` in its frontmatter, review the [agent-scoped hook conversion limitation](/docs/agent-customization/hooks.md#agent-scoped-hooks). Plugin-provided agents can appear and run without their scoped hooks because conversion currently omits the `hooks` field. Retain the scoped definition and leave the affected hook migration incomplete; do not broaden it to repository-wide or plugin-wide execution as a workaround.

 ## Change the model for chat

 Use the language model picker in the chat input field to change the model for chat conversations and code editing.
 
 ![Screenshot that shows the model picker in the Chat view.](images/language-models/model-dropdown-change-model-v2.png)
 
-Different models have different strengths. Use a fast model for quick edits and simple questions, and a reasoning model for complex refactoring, architectural decisions, or multi-step tasks. Depending on the [harness](/docs/agents/concepts/agent-harnesses.md) you are using, the list of available models might differ.
+If the language model picker only shows **Auto**, verify that the workspace is trusted. Learn more about [workspace trust and restricted mode](/docs/editing/workspaces/workspace-trust.md#restricted-mode).
 
 You can further extend the list of available models by [using your own language model API key](#bring-your-own-language-model-key).

-> In an untrusted workspace in [Restricted Mode](/docs/editing/workspaces/workspace-trust.md#restricted-mode), the chat model picker only shows **Auto**. Trust the workspace to restore the full model list.
-
-> [!TIP]
-> Install the AI Toolkit extension to add more language models to enhance GitHub Copilot capabilities.
->
-> For more information, see [Change the chat model](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/how-tos/use-ai-models/change-the-chat-model#adding-more-models).
-
-> [!NOTE]
-> If you are a Copilot Business or Enterprise user, your administrator needs to enable certain models for your organization by opting in to `Editor Preview Features` in the [Copilot policy settings](https://docs.github.com/en/enterprise-cloud@latest/copilot/managing-copilot/managing-github-copilot-in-your-organization/managing-policies-for-copilot-in-your-organization#enabling-copilot-features-in-your-organization) on GitHub.com.
+> If you are a Copilot Business or Copilot Enterprise user, your administrator needs to enable certain models for your organization by opting in to `Editor Preview Features` in the [Copilot policy settings](https://docs.github.com/en/enterprise-cloud@latest/copilot/managing-copilot/managing-github-copilot-in-your-organization/managing-policies-for-copilot-in-your-organization#enabling-copilot-features-in-your-organization) on GitHub.com.
+
+To compare solutions to the same coding task, use [model comparison (Preview)](/docs/agents/run/compare-models.md) in the Agents window. Run one prompt with multiple models or harnesses, then review the results side by side.
 
 ## Configure thinking effort
 
@@ -69,6 +65,113 @@
 You can see which model is used for generating a response by hovering over the chat response.
 
 ![Screenshot of a chat response, showing the selected model on hover.](images/language-models/chat-response-selected-model.png)
+
+## Use on-device models
+
+`feature(on-device-models)`
+
+On-device models let you run a coding model provided by Copilot, currently MAI Code 1.1 Flash, directly on your device in Copilot harness sessions. VS Code uses [llama.cpp](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp) to serve the model on your device. On-device models bring that capability to Copilot in VS Code, without you having to find, download, and serve a model yourself.
+
+You can use the local model alongside cloud models. [Auto-model selection](#use-auto-model-selection) can route suitable requests to the local model, with fallback to a cloud model.
+
+On-device models differ from [BYOK local models](#bring-your-own-language-model-key). Copilot provides and manages on-device models, and they require GitHub sign-in. BYOK local models, such as Ollama, work without sign-in through a provider that you configure.
+
+<video src="images/language-models/on-device-model.mp4" title="Video showing MAI Code 1.1 Flash downloaded from the On This Device tab in the model picker, loaded, and answering a chat prompt on the device." autoplay loop controls muted></video>
+
+### On-device model requirements
+
+* Sign in to GitHub with Copilot access. If you use Copilot Business or Enterprise, your administrator must enable `Editor Preview Features` in the [Copilot policy settings](https://docs.github.com/en/enterprise-cloud@latest/copilot/managing-copilot/managing-github-copilot-in-your-organization/managing-policies-for-copilot-in-your-organization#enabling-copilot-features-in-your-organization).
+
+* Use a [Copilot harness session](/docs/agents/run/agent-harnesses.md) that runs on your local machine in the Chat view or [Agents window](/docs/agents/run/agents-window.md).
+
+* Use an NVIDIA GPU with 96 GB or more of GPU memory (VRAM). Devices with unified memory, memory that the GPU shares with the system, count toward this requirement.
+
+* Have about 55 GB of free disk space for MAI Code 1.1 Flash. The download is 52.8 GB, and setup requires 54.8 GB of free space. The details page in the model picker shows the exact size for your device.
+
+* Install a llama.cpp build that supports the model. VS Code does not install llama.cpp. Get a build from the [llama.cpp releases page](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/releases). MAI Code 1.1 Flash requires llama.cpp b11658 or later. If the build is too old, the model picker shows notification that a newer version is needed.
+
+### Set up an on-device model
+
+1. Install a llama.cpp build for your platform and GPU.
+
+1. Set ``chat.copilot.localModel.llamaServerPath`` to the absolute path of the `llama-server` executable. This setting is machine-scoped. VS Code only runs the `llama-server` executable at this path.
+
+1. Enable ``chat.copilot.localModel.enabled`` `feature(on-device-models)`. When you change this setting, the local Copilot runtime restarts after active responses finish.
+
+1. In a Copilot harness session, open the model picker and select the **On This Device** tab. Models that are not downloaded appear dimmed with a **Download** action.
+
+    ![Screenshot showing the On This Device tab in the model picker with MAI Code 1.1 Flash and a Download action.](images/language-models/on-device-model-picker.png)
+
+1. Select the model name to open its details, review the download size, source (Azure ML registry), and license, and then select **Download**.
+
+    ![Screenshot showing the details page for MAI Code 1.1 Flash with the download size, source, license, and a Download button.](images/language-models/on-device-model-download-details.png)
+
+1. Track progress in the notice above the chat input. You can keep using Copilot while the model downloads, and select **Pause** to pause the download.
+
+    ![Screenshot showing a download progress notice for MAI Code 1.1 Flash above the chat input with a Pause button.](images/language-models/on-device-model-download-progress.png)
+
+VS Code stores models in `~/.copilot/azureml-models` and shares them with other Copilot apps, such as Copilot CLI.
+
+### Use an on-device model in chat
+
+After the download finishes, VS Code verifies the files and loads the model. Loading can take a few minutes, and the notice above the chat input shows the progress, with a **Cancel** action. When the model is ready, select **Switch to MAI Code 1.1 Flash** in the notice, or select the model from the **On This Device** tab.
+
+![Screenshot showing a notice above the chat input that says MAI Code 1.1 Flash is ready on this device, with Not Now and Switch to MAI Code 1.1 Flash buttons.](images/language-models/on-device-model-ready.png)
+
+When you select an on-device model, the model picker and the footer of each response show the model name followed by **On-device**. On-device responses don't use AI credits, and the response footer shows **0 credits**.
+
+![Screenshot showing a chat response labeled MAI Code 1.1 Flash On-device with 0 credits, and the model picker showing the on-device model.](images/language-models/on-device-model-response.png)
+
+To change how the model runs, hover over the model in the **On This Device** tab and select **Details**. On the details page, you can:
+
+* Choose the **Effort Level**: **Low**, **Medium**, or **High**.
+* Choose the **Context** size. The available sizes depend on the GPU memory of your device, and the page shows the estimated memory for the selected size. Changing the context size reloads the model after running responses finish.
+* Stop or remove the model by using the icons at the top of the page.
+
+![Screenshot showing the details page of an installed on-device model with Effort Level, Context, and Use in Auto options.](images/language-models/on-device-model-details.png)
+
+### Use an on-device model with auto-model selection
+
+[Auto-model selection](#use-auto-model-selection) can route requests to the active on-device model, with cloud fallback. If the on-device model can't respond, model selection falls back to a cloud model before the first output. The change takes effect immediately.
+
+To enable the local model for auto-model selection, enable **Use in Auto** on the model's details page, or enable ``chat.copilot.localModel.useInAuto``.
+
+Auto-model selection chooses the appropriate model for each request, so many requests continue to use cloud models. Responses that use the local model are labeled **On-device**. If a model is too slow for auto-model selection on your device, it shows **Too slow for Auto**. You can still select the model directly.
+
+> [!CAUTION]
+> Local models might not use the same safety filters as GitHub Copilot-provided cloud models and might be more susceptible to malicious instructions, especially when [actions are automatically approved](/docs/agents/run/approvals.md).
+
+### Manage on-device models
```

 # Migrate customizations to the Copilot harness in VS Code
 
-When VS Code shows a customization migration notice, it found a file, setting, or MCP server that the Copilot harness does not read in its current location or format. Use the migration list to move or convert each affected customization.
-
-This article explains why migration is needed, how to complete each migration that VS Code supports, and what to review manually.
+Move existing customizations to the Copilot harness, including customizations that require manual changes. When VS Code shows a customization migration notice, use the migration list to move or convert the detected files, settings, or MCP servers.
+
+This article covers supported migrations, [manual hook migration](#migrate-hooks), and replacements for [deprecated tool sets](#replace-tool-sets) and [chat participants](#replace-chat-participants). Hooks can come from user folders, settings, repositories, custom agents, or plugins. These manual migrations do not appear as automatic conversions in the migration list.
 
 > [!NOTE]
 > This article covers migration to the Copilot harness. For the Claude or Codex harness, use the locations and formats in the provider documentation.
+>
+> Claude and Codex harnesses are disabled when [Copilot enterprise-managed settings apply](/docs/enterprise/manage-ai-settings.md#claude-and-codex-availability). Contact the IT team that deployed the settings to discuss access.
 
 ## Open the migration list
 
@@ -180,10 +182,397 @@
 The migration list does not automatically convert every customization type:
 
 * **Custom agents with Local harness-specific behavior**: use **Migrate with Agent** when an agent depends on Local harness tool names, tool sets, hooks, or other behavior that needs semantic changes.
-* **Tool sets**: The Copilot harness does not support VS Code tool-set files. There is no automatic migration. Review the tools directly in the custom agent or chat tools picker. See [Create and use tool sets](/docs/agent-customization/tool-sets.md).
-* **Hooks**: there is no automatic hook migration. Configure hooks for the Copilot harness using the [GitHub Copilot hooks reference](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/hooks-reference) and [Configure agent hooks](/docs/agent-customization/hooks.md).
+* **Tool sets**: deprecated and not supported in the Copilot harness. [Replace tool-set references](#replace-tool-sets) with tools configured for the workflow.
+* **Chat participants**: deprecated and not supported in the Copilot harness. [Replace participant capabilities](#replace-chat-participants) with plugins that combine skills, MCP tools, and, when needed, MCP Apps.
+* **Hooks**: there is no automatic hook migration. Follow [Migrate hooks](#migrate-hooks) to review discovery, command configuration, script inputs, and output decisions.
 * **Plugins and marketplaces**: manage these separately from the migration list. See [Discover and install plugins](/docs/agent-customization/agent-plugins.md#discover-and-install-plugins).
 * **Extension-provided customizations**: extensions can provide tools, MCP servers, custom agents, skills, and instructions. Keep the contributing extension installed. Extension tools are available only in editor chat while the extension runs. Learn how to [manage extension tools for the Copilot harness](/docs/agents/run/tools.md#add-extension-tools-for-the-copilot-harness).
+
+## Migrate hooks
+
+Migrate a hook's configuration **and** its script. A file can load in the Copilot harness while its script no longer matches a tool, reads the wrong arguments, or returns an output the destination does not use. Copying a file or changing an event name alone does not verify a migration.
+
+This procedure applies to personal hooks as well as shared hooks. VS Code, the GitHub Copilot app, and GitHub Copilot CLI use the same Copilot harness and hook format. The migration is from the older Local hook contract to that shared contract, not to a command-line tool.
+
+The [Copilot hooks reference](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/hooks-reference) documents the shared configuration and command-hook payloads. The [Local hooks reference](/docs/agents/reference/hooks-reference.md) describes the source format for an existing Local hook.
+
+### Prepare hooks for migration
+
+Before editing a configuration or starting a Copilot session:
+
+1. Back up the original configuration **and** its scripts, including user settings that are not in source control.
+1. Record which hooks are enabled, disabled, or excluded by Local discovery settings. `chat.useHooks`, `chat.useClaudeHooks`, and `chat.hookFilesLocations` do not preserve those choices in the Copilot harness. A previously ignored file can become active without being moved.
+1. Keep unvalidated configurations outside discovered sources. For example, stage a standalone file with a `.json.disabled` suffix, and use a new script filename rather than overwriting the original. For inline settings, prepare the new `hooks` entries separately before merging them.
+1. Preserve intentionally disabled hooks using the destination's controls. Do not activate the original and converted configurations together.
+
+For standalone Copilot hook files, top-level `disableAllHooks: true` skips only the hooks in that file. In repository Copilot settings, the same property skips hooks from all non-policy sources for that repository. Policy hooks are not disabled by this control. See [disabling hooks](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/hooks-reference#disable-all-hooks), and coordinate managed-hook changes with your administrator.
+
+### Find every hook source
+
+Inventory each configuration and the scripts it invokes before changing anything. Preserve personal hooks at user scope and shared hooks with their repository or plugin.
+
+| Source | What to review for the Copilot harness |
+|--------|---------------------------------------|
+| Repository hook files | Use `.github/hooks/*.json` at the repository root. Review the script paths as well as the JSON. |
+| User hook files | Use `*.json` files under `~/.copilot/hooks/`, or `%USERPROFILE%\.copilot\hooks\` on Windows. Copilot uses `$COPILOT_HOME/hooks/` when `COPILOT_HOME` is set. Check the destination process's home directory. |
+| Additional Local locations | `chat.hookFilesLocations` does not configure Copilot discovery. Move the configuration and its dependencies to a supported source, or package shared hooks in a plugin. |
+| Repository settings | The Copilot harness reads an inline `hooks` map from `.github/copilot/settings.json` and `.github/copilot/settings.local.json`. It also reads repository `.claude/settings.json` and `.claude/settings.local.json`. Preserve other settings; do not replace a settings file with a standalone hook-file envelope. |
+| User settings | Copilot reads inline hooks from `~/.copilot/settings.json`, not `~/.copilot/config.json`. Do not assume a Local hook in `~/.claude/settings.json` is discovered by Copilot. Move it to a documented Copilot user source. |
+| Custom agent frontmatter | Review the agent's `hooks` map and the [agent-scoped hook contract](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/hooks-reference#agent-scoped-hooks). VS Code currently drops this field when converting plugin-provided agents. For those agents, keep the hook definition but leave its migration incomplete; see the [conversion limitation](/docs/agent-customization/hooks.md#agent-scoped-hooks). Copilot also excludes scoped hooks from remote or organization-hosted agent definitions. |
+| Installed plugins | Update the plugin's source package or obtain an updated version from its publisher. Do not patch an installation cache. Keep scripts with the plugin and review its path variables, events, and payloads. See [hooks in plugins](/docs/agents/reference/agent-plugin-reference.md#hooks-in-plugins). |
+| Administrator-managed hooks | Coordinate changes with the administrator. Policy hooks and managed plugin hooks have different discovery and trust rules from personal files. See [enterprise hook controls](/docs/enterprise/manage-ai-settings.md#enable-or-disable-hooks). |
+
+The Copilot harness combines applicable hooks from multiple sources; adding a repository copy does not replace a user or plugin copy. Avoid running both the original and migrated hook. For a shared setup used across repositories, consider distributing the converted hooks and scripts in a plugin rather than copying them into each repository.
+
+Plugin-wide hooks and hooks in a plugin-provided agent's frontmatter are separate configurations. Packaging an agent does not work around the conversion limitation for its scoped hooks. Do not broaden a scoped hook to plugin-wide or repository-wide execution merely to make it run.
+
+### Compare configuration and script contracts
+
+The following table compares the [Local implementation](https://github.com/microsoft/vscode/blob/1.142.0/src/vs/workbench/contrib/chat/common/promptSyntax/hookSchema.ts) with the documented [Copilot command-hook contract](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/hooks-reference#command-hooks). Compatibility aliases do not make the two execution environments identical.
+
+| Area | Existing Local hook | Copilot migration action |
+|------|---------------------|--------------------------|
+| File format | A `hooks` event map, commonly with PascalCase names. Local also accepts Copilot event and command aliases. | Use the documented standalone JSON format with `version: 1`, or an inline `hooks` map in a supported settings file. |
+| Command selection | `command`, with `windows`, `linux`, and `osx` overrides. Local also accepts `bash` and `powershell`. | Standalone command hooks document `command` as a fallback and `bash` or `powershell` as platform commands. Preserve separate Linux and macOS behavior in a script if needed. Agent-scoped hooks also document `windows`, `linux`, and `osx`; do not assume identical field support for every source. |
+| Arguments | Event data arrives on stdin, not as positional command arguments. | Continue reading JSON from stdin. `exec` with `args` is a separate, shell-free invocation option, not an event-payload mapping. Do not combine it with shell command fields. |
+| Working directory | Relative `cwd` resolves from the workspace root; Local also resolves absolute paths and `~`. | Copilot supports repository-relative or absolute `cwd`. Recheck paths when moving a user hook, plugin, or workspace to a remote host or worktree. |
+| Environment | `env` adds values to the extension host's inherited environment. | Review the destination's inherited variables and documented expansion rules. Neither `env` nor `cwd` grants additional sandbox access. |
+| Timeout | Default 30 seconds. Local prefers `timeout` over `timeoutSec` if both are present. | Default 30 seconds. Copilot prefers `timeoutSec` over its `timeout` alias. Specify only one field. |
+| Common input | ISO timestamp, `session_id`, `hook_event_name`, and optional transcript information. | camelCase events use `sessionId` and a numeric epoch-millisecond `timestamp`. PascalCase aliases select compatible snake_case fields and ISO timestamps. |
+| Tool input | `tool_name`, `tool_input`, and `tool_use_id`. | camelCase events use `toolName` and `toolArgs`. PascalCase `PreToolUse` can report names such as `Read`, `Write`, or `Edit` instead of runtime names. Inspect the actual tool arguments; names and schemas are not a one-to-one Local mapping. |
+| Tool result | `PostToolUse` receives `tool_response`. | `postToolUse` receives `toolResult`; the compatible format uses `tool_result`. Rewrite result readers, not just input readers. |
+| Tool decisions | Nested `hookSpecificOutput.permissionDecision` and optional `updatedInput`. | Use the documented top-level `permissionDecision`, `permissionDecisionReason`, and `modifiedArgs`. Return `{}` when there is no hook decision, rather than granting approval with `allow`. |
+| Post-tool context | `hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext`. | Return top-level `additionalContext`. A post-tool hook runs after the action; it cannot undo the completed action. |
+| Exit codes | Exit `2` is blocking. Other nonzero exits produce a nonblocking warning. | GitHub documents command `preToolUse` errors and nonzero exits as denying the tool, but timeouts as continuing through normal permissions. Other events have different failure behavior. Test each event's error path. |
+
+For complete output and failure rules, see [Copilot decision control](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/hooks-reference#pretooluse-decision-control) and [command-hook exit codes](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/hooks-reference#exit-codes-for-command-hooks).
+
+### Review event timing
+
+Use this table to find the corresponding lifecycle point, not as a mechanical rename list. Changing a configured event from PascalCase to camelCase also changes its stdin payload.
+
+| Local event | Copilot event | Behavior to verify |
+|-------------|---------------|--------------------|
+| `SessionStart` | `sessionStart` | Copilot includes new and resumed sessions. Do not assume `source` is always `new`. |
+| `UserPromptSubmit` | `userPromptSubmitted` | Runs for a submitted prompt. Command-hook output is ignored for this event; programmatic SDK prompt rewriting is not a shell-hook feature. |
+| `PreToolUse` | `preToolUse` | Runs before a tool executes. Verify the tool name, argument schema, and permission result. |
+| `PostToolUse` | `postToolUse` | Runs after a successful tool. Copilot has a separate `postToolUseFailure` event. |
+| `PreCompact` | `preCompact` | Copilot documents manual and automatic triggers. It is a notification, not a way to prevent compaction. |
+| `SubagentStart` | `subagentStart` | Review the agent identity fields and which subagent types emit events. The hook reference excludes the built-in `general-purpose` agent. |
+| `SubagentStop` | `subagentStop` | Review response and identity fields, block decisions, and continuation limits. |
+| `Stop` | `agentStop` | The agent is finishing a turn. This is **not** `sessionEnd`, which indicates session termination. |
+
+### Handle fields without a documented equivalent
+
+Do not carry these assumptions into a migrated script:
+
+* **Common Local outputs:** `continue`, `stopReason`, and `systemMessage` are not a documented cross-event Copilot command-hook contract. Choose the destination's event-specific output. Do not treat denying one tool as stopping the whole session.
+* **Nested output envelopes:** accepting a PascalCase input alias does not establish support for every Local `hookSpecificOutput` field. Emit the documented Copilot output.
+* **Transcripts and tool IDs:** do not require `transcript_path` or `tool_use_id` on every event. Copilot documents transcript paths on particular lifecycle events, not all tool events. A transcript filename or substring is not proof that the agent read or followed an instruction.
+* **Matcher behavior:** Local ignores matcher values. Copilot applies documented matcher rules, including different semantics for compatible `PreToolUse` names. Recheck every filter, including invalid patterns and tools it should not match.
+* **SDK callback types:** programmatic callbacks are not JSON-file command hooks. A field in SDK source, such as a callback-only prompt override, is not evidence that a shell hook can use it.
+* **Enforcement:** `ask` depends on an interactive approval flow; do not assume it pauses an unattended session. GitHub also documents fail-open timeouts, including for policy hooks. Neither instructions nor a migrated hook alone establishes a security boundary.
+
+### Example conversion
```

```diff
--- microsoft_vscode-docs::docs/agent-customization/tool-sets.md (prev: 7f34fa90)
+++ microsoft_vscode-docs::docs/agent-customization/tool-sets.md (new: bf217a3d)
@@ -1,61 +1,20 @@
-**MetaDescription**: Group tools for Local harness prompts and custom agents with reusable tool sets in Visual Studio Code.
+**MetaDescription**: Replace deprecated tool sets with custom-agent tool selections and plugins in Visual Studio Code.
 
-# Create and use tool sets
+# Tool sets (deprecated)
 
-A tool set is a reusable collection of agent tools that you can reference as a single entity. This article describes user-defined tool-set files for organizing related tools in prompts, [prompt files](/docs/agent-customization/prompt-files.md), and [custom agents](/docs/agent-customization/custom-agents.md) in Local sessions.
+User-defined tool sets group existing tools under a reusable name. They are deprecated and are not supported in the Copilot harness. Tool-set files remain available only for Local harness compatibility.
 
-> [!IMPORTANT]
-> User-defined tool-set files are supported only in **Local** harness sessions. For the **Copilot** harness, configure tools through its tool controls or a custom agent's tool list instead. See [customizations without automatic migration](/docs/agent-customization/migrate-customizations.md#customizations-without-automatic-migration).
+To move an existing workflow to the Copilot harness, follow [Replace tool sets](/docs/agent-customization/migrate-customizations.md#replace-tool-sets).
 
-VS Code also provides predefined tool sets, such as `#edit` and `#search`.
+## Choose a replacement
 
-## Create a tool set
+* For a recurring role, specify supported tools directly in a [custom agent](/docs/agent-customization/custom-agents.md).
+* For personal tool availability, use the [Tools tab in the Agent Customizations editor](/docs/agents/run/tools.md#manage-available-tools).
+* To distribute a workflow with external tools, package skills and MCP servers in an [agent plugin](/docs/agent-customization/agent-plugins.md).
 
-Select **Local** as the **Session Target** in the Chat view, then create a tool set:
-
-1. Run **Chat: Configure Tool Sets** from the Command Palette and select **Create new tool sets file**.
-
-    Alternatively, select the ellipsis (**...**) menu in the Chat view, select **Tool Sets**, and then select **Create new tool sets file**.
-
-1. Define your tool set in the `.jsonc` file that opens.
-
-    The following example creates a tool set named `reader`:
-
-    ```json
-    {
-        "reader": {
-            "tools": [
-                "search/changes",
-                "search/codebase",
-                "read/problems",
-                "search/usages"
-            ],
-            "description": "Tools for reading and gathering context",
-            "icon": "book"
-        }
-    }
-    ```
-
-    A tool set has the following properties:
-
-    * `tools`: array of built-in, MCP, or extension tool names.
-    * `description`: brief description displayed in the tools picker.
-    * `icon`: icon for the tool set. See the [Product Icon Reference](/api/references/icons-in-labels.md).
-
-## Use a tool set
-
-Reference a tool set in a prompt by typing `#` followed by its name:
-
-```prompt
-Analyze the codebase for security issues #reader
-```
-
-In the tools picker, tool sets appear as collapsible groups. Select or deselect a tool set to change the availability of all its tools at once.
-
-You can also reference tool sets in [prompt files](/docs/agent-customization/prompt-files.md) and [custom agents](/docs/agent-customization/custom-agents.md).
+The tool groups displayed by the Copilot harness and its supported built-in tool aliases are not user-defined tool-set files. A plugin or skill does not recreate a named tool-set alias.
 
 ## Related resources
 
-* [Use tools with agents](/docs/agents/run/tools.md)
-* [Add MCP servers](/docs/agent-customization/mcp-servers.md)
-* [Create custom agents](/docs/agent-customization/custom-agents.md)
+* [Migrate existing customizations](/docs/agent-customization/migrate-customizations.md)
+* [Tools and context reference](/docs/agents/reference/tools-reference.md)
```

```diff
--- microsoft_vscode-docs::docs/agents/best-practices.md (prev: b0dc7a71)
+++ microsoft_vscode-docs::docs/agents/best-practices.md (new: 1d8f3b0c)
@@ -154,6 +154,9 @@
 
 When working with agents, choose the harness that matches your task and workflow. Each harness offers different provider capabilities, tools, and execution environments.
 
+> [!NOTE]
+> Claude and Codex harnesses are disabled when [Copilot enterprise-managed settings apply](/docs/enterprise/manage-ai-settings.md#claude-and-codex-availability). Contact the IT team that deployed the settings to discuss access.
+
 * **Use the Copilot harness for day-to-day coding.** The Copilot harness runs tools on your machine with access to your workspace and run-time context. It is a good default for most coding tasks. Tool execution location is separate from where the selected language model runs.
 
 * **Use Claude or Codex for provider-specific capabilities.** These harnesses also run tools on your machine and provide their own SDK capabilities through the same VS Code session experience.
```

 An agent harness is the runtime that coordinates the agent loop. It manages the session, connects the language model to tools, and provides provider-specific capabilities. VS Code supports harnesses such as Copilot, Claude, and Codex.
 
+> [!NOTE]
+> Claude and Codex harnesses are disabled when [Copilot enterprise-managed settings apply](/docs/enterprise/manage-ai-settings.md#claude-and-codex-availability). Contact the IT team that deployed the settings to discuss access.
+
 The execution environment is where the harness runs workspace tools and changes code. Depending on the available harness, this can be your machine, a connected host, a Dev Container on either host, or cloud infrastructure. A Dev Container provides the project's configured tools and dependencies without changing the harness. Learn more about [agent harnesses and execution environments](/docs/agents/concepts/agent-harnesses.md#relate-execution-environments-and-code-isolation).
 
 ## Capabilities that extend agent work

### [MODIFICADO] `microsoft_vscode-docs::docs/agents/concepts/agent-host.md`
- **URL Canónica**: https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/agents/concepts/agent-host
- **Hash Anterior**: `e85f19f5c2ac94dafc181de7d936040dfb4864bb6c272460d01e394d31fe48b1`
- **Hash Nuevo**: `34a95a9d6f379705640678ffc8209bfb5e093ca4d7f37f29edc092aed6c984bd`

```diff
--- microsoft_vscode-docs::docs/agents/concepts/agent-host.md (prev: e85f19f5)
+++ microsoft_vscode-docs::docs/agents/concepts/agent-host.md (new: 34a95a9d)
@@ -5,8 +5,6 @@
 The Agent Host is a dedicated process that runs agent harnesses and manages their sessions. Separating session execution from an individual window lets you work with the same session across interfaces and run agents close to your code and development tools.
 
 This article explains the process boundaries, execution environments, session lifetime, and client dependencies for integrations that use the Agent Host. You don't need this architecture background to use agents. To identify the harness a session uses or compare Local and Copilot, see [Choose an agent harness](/docs/agents/run/agent-harnesses.md). For session-management tasks, see [Manage sessions](/docs/agents/run/sessions/manage-sessions.md).
-
-<a name="why-a-dedicated-agent-host"></a>
 
 ## What this architecture enables
 
@@ -17,6 +15,9 @@
 * **Independent execution**: an agent session can continue without a connected editor while the host and its execution environment remain running. Tools provided by a client still require that client.
 * **Multiple agent implementations**: different agent runtimes plug into one host-facing interface and present common session concepts to clients.
 * **Dedicated process**: agents run in their own process, where they won't be blocked by busy extensions.
+
+> [!NOTE]
+> Claude and Codex harnesses are disabled when [Copilot enterprise-managed settings apply](/docs/enterprise/manage-ai-settings.md#claude-and-codex-availability). Contact the IT team that deployed the settings to discuss access.
 
 ## Process architecture
 
@@ -33,8 +34,6 @@
 The Agent Host can run as a local utility process or as a standalone server on a remote machine. VS Code uses a message port for local IPC and AHP JSON-RPC over WebSocket for remote connections.
 
 Agent adapters run inside the Agent Host process. An adapter translates between its agent runtime and the common [Agent Host Protocol (AHP)](#agent-host-protocol) session model.
-
-<a name="local-and-remote-hosts"></a>
 
 ## Where sessions run
 
@@ -55,8 +54,6 @@
 Like [VS Code Remote Development](/docs/remote/remote-overview.md), the user interface stays on the client while workspace operations run close to the source code and development tools.
 
 The **Cloud** target is a separate choice that uses provider-managed infrastructure. Connecting to an Agent Host on another machine does not turn the session into a cloud-agent session.
-
-<a name="self-contained-with-optional-client-tools"></a>
 
 ## Session lifetime and client dependencies
 
@@ -79,8 +76,6 @@
 ## Run a standalone host
 
 To run your own standalone Agent Host, use `code agent host`. By default, the command starts a server on localhost and protects it with a connection token. Use the `--tunnel` option to expose it through a dev tunnel.
-
-<a name="behavior-on-the-extension-host"></a>
 
 ## Local and existing extension-host sessions
 
```

 Sessions can run in parallel and keep running when you switch between them. To return an existing conversation and affected workspace files to an earlier point instead of branching the conversation, see [Restore a checkpoint](/docs/agents/run/review-code-edits.md#restore-a-checkpoint).
 
 ## Sessions across surfaces
+
+> [!NOTE]
+> Claude and Codex harnesses are disabled when [Copilot enterprise-managed settings apply](/docs/enterprise/manage-ai-settings.md#claude-and-codex-availability). Existing sessions are not deleted by this restriction. Contact the IT team that deployed the settings to discuss access.
 
 You can access and continue the same live **Copilot** or **Claude** session in the [Chat view](/docs/agents/run/chat-view.md) and the [Agents window](/docs/agents/run/agents-window.md). To use **Codex** in both interfaces, configure the [experimental Codex integration](/docs/agents/run/agent-harnesses.md#codex). Switching between these interfaces doesn't create a session, fork the conversation, or change its harness, workspace, or worktree.
 
```

```diff
--- microsoft_vscode-docs::docs/agents/concepts/customization.md (prev: ce4c4067)
+++ microsoft_vscode-docs::docs/agents/concepts/customization.md (new: 62a2280f)
@@ -21,10 +21,10 @@
 * **Define repeatable work**: [agent skills](/docs/agent-customization/agent-skills.md) and [prompt files](/docs/agent-customization/prompt-files.md) package task-specific guidance so you don't have to describe the same process in every conversation.
 * **Configure a role**: [custom agents](/docs/agent-customization/custom-agents.md) combine instructions, tools, and a language model into a specialized agent persona.
 * **Add capabilities**: [MCP servers](/docs/agent-customization/mcp-servers.md) give the agent tools for interacting with external systems such as databases, browsers, and APIs.
-* **Enforce actions**: [hooks](/docs/agent-customization/hooks.md) run commands at specific points in the agent loop, independent of whether the model chooses to run them.
+* **Automate lifecycle actions**: [hooks](/docs/agent-customization/hooks.md) run commands at specific points in the agent loop, independent of whether the model chooses to run them.
 * **Adopt a ready-made setup**: [agent plugins](/docs/agent-customization/agent-plugins.md) let you install multiple related customization types as one package.
 
-These mechanisms affect different layers of the agent. Instructions guide the model's decisions, tools expand the actions it can take, and hooks run outside the model's decision-making. As a result, adding a rule to an instructions file is not equivalent to enforcing that rule with a hook.
+These mechanisms affect different layers of the agent. Instructions guide the model's decisions, tools expand the actions it can take, and hooks run outside the model's decision-making. Adding a rule to an instructions file is not equivalent to executing a validation script. Neither mechanism alone establishes a security boundary.
 
 Relevant customizations can reduce back-and-forth and rework by giving the agent project context from the start. Avoiding corrective turns and discarded implementations can help reduce [AI credit usage](/docs/agents/guides/optimize-usage.md). Keep customizations focused because their content also consumes space in the model's context window.
 
@@ -71,9 +71,11 @@
 
 Most customizations guide the model or give it more options. The model interprets instructions, decides whether a skill is relevant, and chooses when to call an available tool. The result depends on the request, available context, and model reasoning.
 
-Hooks are deterministic. A hook runs when its configured lifecycle event occurs. Use a hook when an action must happen consistently, such as validating a command before it runs or starting a formatter after an edit. Use instructions when you want to guide how the agent reasons or writes code.
+Hooks are dispatched by the runtime when a supported, configured lifecycle event occurs. Use a hook for actions such as validating a tool call or starting a formatter after an edit. Discovery, trust, filters, timeouts, and error handling still affect execution. Use instructions when you want to guide how the agent reasons or writes code.
 
 For example, an instruction that says "run the formatter after editing a file" asks the model to remember and perform that action. A hook configured for the corresponding lifecycle event runs regardless of whether the model remembers the instruction.
+
+For new Copilot hooks, use the [shared runtime guidance](/docs/agent-customization/hooks.md). Existing hooks in personal folders, settings, repositories, agents, or plugins need a [compatibility review](/docs/agent-customization/migrate-customizations.md#migrate-hooks) when migrating from Local.
 
 ## How customizations combine
 
```

 * **Models from your GitHub Copilot plan**: choose from multiple models by different providers, such as Anthropic, Google, and OpenAI, included with your plan.

 This article explains how language models work, their characteristics, and how to think about model selection.

 Auto selects from multiple models and respects your organization's [model access settings](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/how-tos/use-ai-models/configure-access-to-ai-models). Auto won't select models excluded by administrator policies or models restricted by data-residency policies.
 
+When you turn on **Use in Auto** for an [on-device model](/docs/agent-customization/language-models.md#use-on-device-models-experimental), Auto can route some requests to that model on your device, with cloud fallback.

 For more details, see [About Copilot auto model selection](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/concepts/auto-model-selection) in the GitHub documentation.

 ## Manage OTel configuration for your organization
 
-Enterprises can mandate OTel export configuration centrally through Copilot enterprise-managed settings, so that telemetry flows to an approved collector without each developer setting `OTEL_*` environment variables. Managed telemetry configuration applies to both the Copilot Chat extension and the Agent Host process.
+Enterprises can mandate OTel export configuration centrally through Copilot enterprise-managed settings, without each developer setting `OTEL_*` environment variables. Managed telemetry configuration applies to both the Copilot Chat extension and the Agent Host process. In addition to collector export, Copilot Agent Host sessions support a managed file destination.
 
 For delivery methods, configuration fields, client support, and precedence, see [`telemetry` in the enterprise-managed settings reference](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/enterprise-administrators/enterprise-managed-settings#telemetry). For the behavior specific to VS Code, see [Apply managed telemetry in VS Code](/docs/enterprise/manage-ai-settings.md#apply-managed-telemetry-in-vs-code).
 
@@ -289,14 +289,96 @@
 > [!NOTE]
 > When a managed telemetry value is configured, it overrides user settings. Managed identity capture also overrides `COPILOT_OTEL_CAPTURE_IDENTITY`, and managed resource attributes override `OTEL_RESOURCE_ATTRIBUTES`. Remove other conflicting OTel environment variables from managed devices to ensure that the enterprise configuration takes effect in the Copilot Chat extension.
 
-<a name="trace-structure-for-background-and-claude-agents"></a>
+### Export managed telemetry to a file
+
+Starting with VS Code 1.143, administrators can set `telemetry.filePath` to save Copilot Agent Host telemetry as JSON Lines (JSONL), without running an OTLP collector or requiring developers to opt in through personal OTel settings. This managed destination is separate from the Copilot Chat extension's [file exporter](#other-exporter-examples).
+
+For a local Agent Host, add the following telemetry block to the device-wide `managed-settings.json` file, not your editor settings JSON:
+
+| Platform | Managed settings file |
+|---|---|
+| macOS | `/Library/Application Support/GitHubCopilot/managed-settings.json` |
+| Linux | `/etc/github-copilot/managed-settings.json` |
+| Windows | `%ProgramFiles%\GitHubCopilot\managed-settings.json` |
+
+```json
+{
+    "telemetry": {
+        "filePath": "~/copilot-telemetry/copilot-otel.jsonl",
+        "captureContent": false,
+        "capture": {
+            "identity": false
+        }
+    }
+}
+```
+
+Choose an absolute or home-relative output path that the Agent Host can write to. Paths refer to the machine that exports the telemetry. The `~/` prefix expands to that machine's user home directory. Environment-variable references such as `$HOME` or `%USERPROFILE%` in `filePath` are not expanded and are rejected.
+
+Setting `filePath` enables Agent Host OTel export without `enabled: true`. An explicit managed `telemetry.enabled: false` takes precedence and turns off export.
+
+After VS Code resolves a managed telemetry change, it restarts the local Agent Host automatically. Start a new Copilot Agent Host session and send a prompt, then check the destination file for spans such as `invoke_agent` and `vscode.agent_host.session`.
+
+The exporter creates missing parent directories and appends one span per line. If it cannot resolve the path or write to the file, the Agent Host logs an error without blocking chat. It does not fall back to a collector. Apply your organization's access and retention controls to the exported files.
+
+> [!IMPORTANT]
+> A managed `filePath` takes precedence over collector configuration. While it is configured, Agent Host telemetry goes to the file instead of the collector, and [dynamic headers](#configure-dynamic-headers) do not apply. Include the file configuration in the [highest-precedence managed telemetry block](/docs/enterprise/manage-ai-settings.md#apply-managed-telemetry-in-vs-code).
+
+To remove the managed file destination, remove `telemetry.filePath` from the effective managed configuration. After the change is applied, another configured collector or personal Agent Host exporter can become effective again. Removing `filePath` stops all export only when no other exporter is configured. To turn off Agent Host OTel export through managed settings, set `telemetry.enabled` to `false`.
+
+### Configure dynamic headers
+
+Starting with VS Code 1.143, administrators can configure `telemetry.headersProvider` for Copilot Agent Host sessions to generate and refresh HTTP headers for OTLP export. The Agent Host runs a helper executable to obtain header values, so developers don't need to update them manually. Use these headers to supply collector-specific metadata, route telemetry, or authenticate with the collector.
+
+Configure the provider through Copilot enterprise-managed settings, not your editor settings JSON. For example, deploy the following telemetry block in `managed-settings.json`:
+
+```json
+{
+    "telemetry": {
+        "enabled": true,
+        "endpoint": "https://<collector-host>:4318",
+        "protocol": "http/protobuf",
+        "captureContent": false,
+        "capture": {
+            "identity": false
+        },
+        "headers": {
+            "x-organization": "<organization-id>"
+        },
+        "headersProvider": {
+            "path": "<absolute-path-to-header-helper>",
+            "args": [],
+            "refreshIntervalMs": 1740000
+        }
+    }
+}
+```
+
+Replace the endpoint, organization ID, and helper path with your deployment values. Install the helper and its dependencies on the machine where the Agent Host runs. For remote sessions, the executable and paths must exist on the remote host.
+
+The helper runs from the execution host's home directory, with the arguments in `args` passed without shell evaluation. It must finish within 30 seconds and write a JSON object with string values to standard output:
+
+```json
+{
+    "x-team": "platform",
+    "x-environment": "production"
+}
+```
+
+The helper can also return an `Authorization` header when the collector requires authentication.
+
+The provider refreshes headers every 29 minutes by default. Set `refreshIntervalMs` to match how often your header values need to be refreshed. Returning `{}` clears dynamic headers. If a refresh fails, exports that require dynamic headers are blocked until the provider refreshes successfully.
+
+Headers returned by the provider take precedence over managed static `telemetry.headers` values with the same name. Nonconflicting static headers remain in the request. Managed headers also take precedence over user-provided headers.
+
+The provider requires an `endpoint` in the same telemetry block and supports HTTP OTLP export with `http/protobuf` or `http/json`, not gRPC. Managed export does not require developers to opt in through personal OTel settings.
+
+Higher-precedence managed sources replace the entire `telemetry` block. Include all required fields in the winning source rather than splitting the endpoint and provider across delivery methods. See [managed-settings delivery precedence](/docs/enterprise/manage-ai-settings.md#across-managed-settings-delivery-methods) and the [header provider reference](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/copilot-cli-reference/cli-command-reference#dynamic-authentication-headers) for more information.
 
 ## Trace structure by harness
 
 When OTel is enabled, the Copilot and Claude integrations described in this section are automatically instrumented. The same settings used for Local harness session traces also enable traces for these integrations.
 
-<a name="copilot"></a>
-
 ### Copilot harness
 
 The shared runtime for Copilot harness sessions runs in the same VS Code process as the chat extension and produces a rich trace hierarchy that includes subagents, permissions, hooks, and tool calls. The extension wrapper span (`invoke_agent copilotcli`, service `copilot-chat`) parents the runtime's native spans (service `github-copilot`). Both appear in the same trace in your collector.
```

+

 ### Hook file location
 
@@ -307,111 +309,57 @@
     hooks/

-    format.sh            # Hook script referenced by hooks.json
